Traces of Hecke operators via hypergeometric character sums
Jerome W. Hoffman, Wen-Ching Winnie Li, Ling Long, and Fang-Ting Tu

Abstract
. In this paper we obtain explicit formulas for the traces of Hecke operators on spaces of cusp forms in certain instances related to arithmetic triangle groups. These expressions are in terms of hypergeometric character sums over finite fields, a theory developed largely by Greene [ 30 ] , Katz [ 40 ] , Beukers-Cohen-Mellit [ 16 ] , and Fuselier-Long-Ramakrishna-Swisher-Tu [ 27 ] . Our approach, in contrast to the previous works, is uniform and more geometric, and it works equally well for forms on elliptic modular curves and Shimura curves. The same method can be applied to obtain eigenvalues of Hecke operators as well.



1991 Mathematics Subject Classification
11F11, 11F80, 11L05, 33C20

1.
Introduction and main results

1.1.
Introduction

In this paper we obtain explicit formulas for the traces of Hecke operators on spaces of cusp forms in certain instances related to
arithmetic triangle groups. These expressions are in terms of hypergeometric character sums over finite fields, a theory
developed largely by Greene
[
30
]
, Katz
[
40
]
, Beukers-Cohen-Mellit
[
16
]
, and
Fuselier-Long-Ramakrishna-Swisher-Tu
[
27
]
.

Earlier works in this direction include
[
3
]
by Ahlgren and Ono for
\Gamma_{0}(8)
,
[
2
]
by Ahlgren for
\Gamma_{0}(4)
,
[
26
]
by Frechette, Ono and Papanikolas for newforms of
\Gamma_{0}(8)
,
[
28
]
by Fuselier for
\Gamma_{0}(1)
and
[
46
,
47
]
by Lennon for
\Gamma_{0}(3)
and
\Gamma_{0}(9)
. The method in these papers
is to combine (i) counting formulas for elliptic curves over finite fields, due to Schoof
[
61
]
, and (ii)
the Selberg trace formula.
That proof is a descendant of the paper of Ihara
[
37
]
wherein he computed the
traces of Hecke operators for
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbf{Z})
. Ihara did not use character sums and his counting
of elliptic curves was based on results of Deuring. The paper
[
58
]
by Ono and Saad did it for
\Gamma_{0}(2)
and
\Gamma_{0}(4)
, following Zagier’s work
[
73
]
for
\Gamma_{0}(1)
. Their method uses the Rankin-Cohen brackets of Zagier’s mock modular form.

In contrast to the works above, our approach, inspired by
[
49
]
by the last three authors and Scholl
[
60
]
, is more geometric. It is based on Eichler-Shimura theory
[
65
]
as interpreted by Deligne
[
21
]
for elliptic modular groups and by Kuga-Shimura
[
44
]
, Shimura
[
64
]
and Ohta
[
54
,
55
,
56
]
for the quaternion case. Let
\Gamma
be an arithmetic triangle group.
Shimura’s theory of canonical models implies that there is a smooth and projective algebraic curve
X_{\Gamma}
defined over
an number field attached to it. For the groups considered in this paper, the number field is
\mathbf{Q}
. The key point is that the trace of the Hecke operator
T_{p}
acting on
S_{k+2}(\Gamma)
, the space of cusp forms
1
1

1

By a cusp form we mean a holomorphic modular form which vanishes at all cusps of
\Gamma
. In particular, if
\Gamma
is cocompact, then a cusp form is just a modular form.

of weight
k+2
, is equal to the trace of Frobenius
\mathrm{Frob}_{p}
acting
on the
H^{1}
of this curve
X_{\Gamma}
with coefficients in a suitable
\ell
-adic sheaf, denoted by
V^{k}(\Gamma)_{\ell}
; see §
2
for the setup.
The Grothendieck -Lefschetz theorem implies

(1)

-\mathrm{Tr}(T_{p}\mid S_{k+2}(\Gamma))=-\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}_{p}\mid H^{1}(X_{\Gamma},V^{k}(\Gamma)_{\ell}))=\sum_{\lambda\in X_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{F}_{p})}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}_{\lambda}\mid(V^{k}(\Gamma)_{\ell})_{\bar{\lambda}}),

and the main results of this paper express the terms on the right-hand side in terms of hypergeometric character sums attached to a hypergeometric datum
HD(\Gamma)
.

The novelty of this approach is twofold:

1.

We give a unified treatment for
\Gamma
elliptic modular and
\Gamma
arising from quaternion algebras, specifically the quaternion algebra over
\mathbf{Q}
of discriminant 6. The latter continues the study of 4-dimensional Galois representations admitting quaternionic multiplications as
initiated in
[
9
,
48
]
. We make use of a certain family of abelian varieties with quaternion structures proposed in
[
20
]
and we extend some of the results of
[
49
]
. Moreover, if
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a congruence subgroup of a group
\Gamma
studied by our approach such that
X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
is defined over
\mathbf{Q}
and the canonical covering map
X_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\to X_{\Gamma}
is explicit, then our method also expresses the traces of the Hecke operators on
S_{k+2}(\Gamma^{\prime})
in terms of hypergeometric character sums attached to
HD(\Gamma)
. In particular, we obtain the Hecke traces for the groups cited above this way.

2.

Suppose
S_{k+2}(\Gamma)
is
m
-dimensional. Our method used to compute the right-hand side of (
1
) can be easily extended to obtain the traces of
(\mathrm{Frob}_{p})^{r}
,
1\leq r\leq m
, which in turn give rise to the eigenvalues of
T_{p}
on
S_{k+2}(\Gamma)
. Modular forms for a congruence subgroup arising from a quaternion algebra over
\mathbf{Q}
do not admit the usual Fourier expansion. This has been a handicap to understanding the arithmetic of modular forms on Shimura curves, as showcased in Yang’s paper
[
72
]
, where examples of Hecke eigenvalues for small primes are computed. Our method opens up a door to explore this territory.

1.2.
Main results

The arithmetic triangle groups

(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})=\langle g_{1},g_{2}\mid g_{1}^{e_{1}}=g_{2}^{e_{2}}=(g_{1}g_{2})^{e_{3}}=id\rangle

have been classified by Takeuchi,

[
67
]
,
[
68
]
, and each can be realized as a discrete subgroup of
\text{PSL}_{2}(\mathbf{R})
acting on the complex upper half plane
\mathfrak{H}
. The groups we consider are of two types: (1) those with noncompact fundamental regions, and therefore cusps, are commensurable with
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbf{Z})
, and (2) those with compact fundamental regions, and therefore no cusps, are commensurable with
O^{1}_{B_{6}}
, the group of norm-1 elements of a maximal order (which is unique up to conjugation) in the quaternion algebra
B_{6}
over
\mathbf{Q}
of discriminant 6. We also consider related groups gotten by adding Atkin-Lehner involutions.
Note that modular forms on these quaternion groups are more difficult to understand because of the lack of
q
-expansions. In those cases, the
Jacquet-Langlands (JL) correspondence allows one to relate these modular forms to cusp forms on congruence subgroups of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbf{Z})
, and
we have utilized the JL correspondence to verify the calculations done in this paper related to the quaternions. Diagrams of the triangle groups and
their interrelationships appear in Figures 1 and 2. In all cases, the curves
X_{\Gamma}
are of genus 0 and defined over
\mathbf{Q}
.

\begin{diagram}

(a)

Class I groups

\begin{diagram}

(b)

Class II groups

On the right, the group (2,4,6) arises from the indefinite quaternion algebra
B_{6}=\left(\frac{-1,3}{\mathbf{Q}}\right)
with a maximal order
O_{B_{6}}=\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{Z}I+\mathbf{Z}J+\mathbf{Z}\frac{1+I+J+IJ}{2}
, where
I^{2}=-1,J^{2}=3,IJ=-JI
.
Its elliptic points of order
2
,
4
,
6
are the fixed points of the Atkin-Lehner involutions
w_{6}
,
w_{2}
,
w_{3}
, induced from the elements
3I+IJ
,
1+I
,
(3+3I+J+IJ)/2
of
O_{B_{6}}
, respectively. As a group, (2,4,6) is generated by the reduced norm 1 elements of
O_{B_{6}}
together with
w_{2},w_{3}
and
w_{6}
modulo the center.

To all but two triangle groups
\Gamma
on the list, we associate hypergeometric data
HD(\Gamma)=\{\alpha(\Gamma),\beta(\Gamma)\}
defined over
\mathbf{Q}
, for which Beukers-Cohen-Mellit
[
16
]
introduced hypergeometric character sums
H_{p}(HD(\Gamma),\lambda)
for
\lambda\in\mathbf{F}_{p}^{\times}
(cf. §
4.1
). We shall express the trace of
T_{p}
on
S_{k+2}(\Gamma)
in terms of these hypergeometric character sums. Our main result is as follows. For integers
m\geq 1
, let
F_{m}(S,T)
be the degree-
m
polynomial in
S
and
T
defined by the recursive relation

(2)

\displaystyle F_{m+1}(S,T)=(S-T)F_{m}(S,T)-T^{2}F_{m-1}(S,T),\quad F_{0}(S,T)=1,\quad F_{1}(S,T)=S.

Then with
S=u^{2}+uv+v^{2}
and
T=uv
, we have

F_{m}(u^{2}+uv+v^{2},uv)=\sum_{i=0}^{2m}u^{i}v^{2m-i}.

In the latter case, upon picking any prime ideal
\wp

In fact, we may choose

4.1
-§
4.2
for more detail.

Here is an example illustrating our main result.
For
\Gamma=(2,4,6)
the lowest

, in which case

corresponds to the normalized weight-

in the L-functions and Modular Forms Database (LMFDB) label notation, which we will adopt throughout this paper. For primes
p>5
, denote by
a_{p}(h_{4}^{2})
the eigenvalue of
T_{p}
on
h_{4}^{2}
, which is equal to the
p
th Fourier coefficient
a_{p}(f_{6.8.a.a})
of
f_{6.8.a.a}
. The theorem above gives

\displaystyle-a_{p}(h_{4}^{2})=-a_{p}(f_{6.8.a.a})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda\in\mathbf{F}_{p},\lambda\neq 0,1}\left(a_{\Gamma}(\lambda,p)^{3}-2pa_{\Gamma}(\lambda,p)^{2}-p^{2}a_{\Gamma}(\lambda,p)+p^{3}\right)

\displaystyle+p((pH_{p}(HD(\Gamma);1))^{2}-p^{2})+\left(\left(\frac{-1}{p}\right)+\left(\frac{-3}{p}\right)+\left(\frac{-6}{p}\right)\right)p^{3}.

More generally, under the Jacquet-Langlands correspondence, the space
S_{k+2}(2,4,6)
is sent to
S_{k+2}^{new}(\Gamma_{0}(6),-,-)
, the subspace of newforms on
\Gamma_{0}(6)
which are eigenfunctions with eigenvalues

of the Atkin-Lehner operators
\omega_{2}
and
\omega_{3}
, hence we obtain a formula for the traces of Hecke operators on the latter space.

For an index-2 subgroup
\Gamma\in\{(2,6,6),(2,2,2,3),(3,4,4)\}
of
(2,4,6)

-rational covering map

, which in turn gives rise to those for

by inclusion-exclusion. Similar to

to the subspace of

consisting of common eigenfunctions of
\omega_{2}
and

with certain prescribed eigenvalues. See §

for details. In particular, as a corollary of

, the trace of
T_{p}
on
S_{4}(2,2,2,3)

for any prime
